<result>I froze the M1 interfaces for both packages, and `scripts/check.sh gate` passes, including the 14 contract tests.

**Types**
- `packages/mdata/src/mdata/types.py` has `RawRow`, `Prices` and `GapError`.
- `packages/risk/src/risk/types.py` has `Returns`, `VolSeries`, `TickerSummary`, `Drawdown` and `Report`.
- Fields match `contract.md`, including the new `trough_date` on `Drawdown`.

**Stubs**: each signature is in its owner module, with a body that only raises `NotImplementedError` naming the task that replaces it.

| Function | Task |
|---|---|
| `read_csv` | M1-T02 |
| `validate_rows` | M1-T03 |
| `align_calendar` | M1-T04 |
| `load_prices` | M1-T11 |
| `log_returns` | M1-T05 |
| `rolling_vol` | M1-T06 |
| `annualize` | M1-T07 |
| `summary_stats` | M1-T08 |
| `max_drawdown` | M1-T09 |
| `write_report` | M1-T10 |
| `vol_series` | M1-T12 |
| `risk_report` | M1-T13 |

**Exports**: `mdata/__init__.py` and `risk/__init__.py` export exactly the contract symbols. `risk/__init__.py` no longer has its bare `import mdata`.

**Contract folders** (under `tests/contracts/`)
- One `sample.json` per type with the cases the contract names. The Drawdown sample has a `fall` case and a `no_fall` case.
- `mdata.load_prices/fake.py` serves the `mdata.Prices` sample. Next to it, `prices.csv` is the CSV that sample comes from, so the real `load_prices` can later be checked against the fake.
- `risk.vol_series/fake.py` serves the `risk.VolSeries` sample. It raises `ValueError` for a window below 2 and `KeyError` for a ticker it doesn't have.
- `test_contracts.py` checks that each sample fits its type and invariants, that the fakes serve the samples, and that the exports are exactly the contract.

**Decisions to review**
- **Samples computed by hand.** I wasn't allowed to run Python, so the sample values are hand-calculated and rounded, not computed by the real steps. The `risk.Returns` sample shares the dates and tickers of the `mdata.Prices` sample, but its values are round numbers rather than the actual log returns of those prices. A check at milestone exit that compares the real `vol_series` output with its fake's numbers would therefore not match exactly.
- **The `vol_series` fake ignores `window`.** It returns the window-2 sample for any window of 2 or more. So the PL-03 smoke test (M1-T13) gets a `VolSeries` whose `window` is 2 even if it asks for the default of 20.
- **No `schema.json` files.** The contract tests check each sample's shape instead.

<result>I've implemented the spine version of `align_calendar`, and `scripts/check.sh gate` passes: lint, format, mypy, size, deptry, import-linter, 29 unit and contract tests, floor guard, and no protected files changed.

- **What it does:** it keeps only the dates on which every ticker has a non-blank close, with no gap filling. Tickers come out sorted and dates ascending. On the RawRow sample, 2024-01-04 is dropped because BBB's close is blank there.
- **One edge case to know about:** a ticker whose closes are all blank still appears in the output, which leaves the calendar with no dates at all.
- **Not used yet:** the signature is unchanged, but `max_gap` does nothing in this version. Filling gaps and raising `GapError` belong to the thicken step.

The new tests cover the sample, unsorted input, a ticker with only blank closes, and empty input.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I made `align_calendar` fill short gaps with the previous close and raise `GapError` naming the ticker when a gap is too long. TC-02, TC-03 and the rest of the intent tests pass without changes, and `scripts/check.sh gate` passes (85 tests).

- **Calendar:** it starts on the first date where every ticker has a close. From there it includes every date any ticker trades. A missing date takes that ticker's previous close.
- **Gap limit:** a run of more than `max_gap` missing dates in a row raises `GapError`, with the ticker's name in the message. Missing dates at the end of the series count too.
- **Interpretation:** I read "the first date every ticker has a close" as the first date on which all tickers have a close at the same time. That matches TC-02. The other reading, the latest of each ticker's first dates, would differ only when that date isn't shared.
- **Edge case:** if no date is shared by all tickers, the result has no dates, and each ticker gets an empty series. This keeps what the earlier version did.
- **Unit tests:** the old sample test expected the 2024-01-04 row to be dropped. It now expects that date to be kept, with BBB's blank close filled as 20.1. I added tests for where the calendar starts, missing dates at the end, a gap exactly at `max_gap` being filled, a gap past it raising, and the no-shared-date case.

full text
Build M1 has stopped one task short of the end. The last checkpoint review found a Critical bug, and nothing else can start until you decide what to do with it.

**Progress:** 20 of 21 tasks have landed and nothing is running. M1-T21, the system task that runs the golden cases on the finished steps, is waiting on this decision.

**The Critical finding, M1-R12** (`packages/risk/src/risk/drawdown.py:20`, found in the risk-package review after M1-T20):
- **What breaks:** `max_drawdown` builds wealth by adding up log returns, so two peaks at the same price come out a tiny fraction apart. That breaks RISK-05's rule that the first peak wins a tie.
- **Example:** with closes 100, 110, 99, 110, 90, it returns the peak date `d3` where the spec requires `d1`. The depth (−0.1818) is right; only the date is wrong. The same effect can move a tied trough date.
- **Reviewer's proposed fix:** compare with a 1e-12 tolerance, so a new peak counts only when it is higher by more than that, and likewise for a new trough. Add a unit test with those closes that expects `("d1", "d4")`.
- The full review is in `.git/dt-build/reviews/M1-T20.risk.md`.

**Decide one of:**
1. **A fix task:** `build-planning` Replan adds a fix task for M1-R12 before M1-T21. You approve the replanned plan and give a new go, and the build resumes at a cap of 1.
2. **Override:** you accept M1-R12 as it stands. I record that with `dt build resolve M1-R12` and M1-T21 runs next.

**Important findings waiting:** the reviews have also raised 8 Important findings (M1-R03 to M1-R16 include them). They're due at a user gate, so this one counts. If you choose a Replan, it can turn some or all of them into fix tasks at the same time. Should I fold them in, or keep the Replan to M1-R12 only?
